{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-haiku-4-5-20251001","id":"msg_vrtx_019EQ5UF6wpyZ7kSsq8nU2a4","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_vrtx_0141ai7aHvUW9NGiQprmTcF9","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/root/environment/skills/pubchem-api.md","content":"# PubChem API for Chemical Name to SMILES Conversion\n\n## Overview\nPubChem is a free chemistry database that provides programmatic access to convert chemical names to molecular structures (SMILES format).\n\n## Installation\nNo additional installation required - uses `requests` library (usually pre-installed):\n```bash\npip install requests\n```\n\n## Core Concepts\n- **PubChem Compound API**: Provides access to chemical compounds and their properties\n- **Chemical Identifier**: Can search by name, CAS number, or other identifiers\n- **SMILES**: Simplified Molecular Input Line Entry System - a standard notation for chemical structures\n\n## Key API Endpoints\n\n### 1. Search by Name\nGet the compound ID:\n```\nhttps://pubchem.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/rest/v1/compound/name/{name}/cids/json\n```\n\n### 2. Get Compound Details\nRetrieve compound information including SMILES:\n```\nhttps://pubchem.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/rest/v1/compound/{cid}/property/CanonicalSMILES/json\n```\n\n## Usage Example\n\n```python\nimport requests\nimport json\n\ndef get_smiles_from_name(chemical_name):\n    \"\"\"Convert chemical name to SMILES using PubChem API\"\"\"\n    try:\n        # Step 1: Get CID (Compound ID) from chemical name\n        search_url = f\"https://pubchem.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/rest/v1/compound/name/{chemical_name}/cids/json\"\n        search_response = requests.get(search_url)\n        search_response.raise_for_status()\n\n        cids = search_response.json()[\"IdentifierList\"][\"CID\"]\n        if not cids:\n            return None\n\n        # Use the first (most relevant) CID\n        cid = cids[0]\n\n        # Step 2: Get SMILES from CID\n        props_url = f\"https://pubchem.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/rest/v1/compound/{cid}/property/CanonicalSMILES/json\"\n        props_response = requests.get(props_url)\n        props_response.raise_for_status()\n\n        smiles = props_response.json()[\"PropertyTable\"][\"Compounds\"][0][\"CanonicalSMILES\"]\n        return smiles\n\n    except Exception as e:\n        print(f\"Error fetching SMILES for {chemical_name}: {e}\")\n        return None\n\n# Example usage\nsmiles = get_smiles_from_name(\"benzene\")\nprint(smiles)  # Output: c1ccccc1\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n\n1. **Error Handling**: Always wrap API calls in try-except blocks\n2. **Caching**: Cache results to avoid repeated API calls for the same chemical\n3. **Rate Limiting**: PubChem allows ~5 requests per second per IP\n4. **Normalization**: Chemical names can have variations - the API handles most common forms\n\n## Advantages\n- Free and no authentication required\n- Comprehensive database of organic and inorganic compounds\n- Canonical SMILES output is standardized and reproducible\n- Handles common chemical name variations\n\n## Limitations\n- May not find very obscure or proprietary compound names\n- API calls have slight latency (good to cache results)\n- Some compounds may have multiple SMILES representations (API provides canonical form)\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":5,"cache_creation_input_tokens":126,"cache_read_input_tokens":19597,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":126,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":1},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"8e28df1d-f990-4c35-90b6-476369423727","uuid":"ff14c6d9-f119-48a4-9c68-4b75dd4d2ab2"}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-haiku-4-5-20251001","id":"msg_vrtx_01SWDNLVonySSxTT3Ad3tfUK","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_vrtx_01Sb1nCDE4rnHpzXMW2tAbkL","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/root/environment/skills/rdkit-morgan-fingerprints.md","content":"# RDKit Morgan Fingerprints and Tanimoto Similarity\n\n## Overview\nRDKit (React Development Kit) is a powerful chemistry informatics library that can convert SMILES to molecular objects and compute structural similarity using Morgan fingerprints.\n\n## Installation\n```bash\npip install rdkit\n```\n\n## Core Concepts\n\n### 1. Molecule Objects\nRDKit converts SMILES strings to `Mol` objects that represent molecular structures:\n```python\nfrom rdkit import Chem\n\nmol = Chem.MolFromSmiles(\"c1ccccc1\")  # benzene\n```\n\n### 2. Morgan Fingerprints\nMorgan fingerprints are circular fingerprints that capture molecular connectivity information:\n- **Radius**: How many layers of atoms to consider (typical: 2-3)\n- **Chirality**: Whether to distinguish stereochemistry (important for pharmaceutical molecules)\n- **Bit Vector**: Binary representation of molecular features\n\n### 3. Tanimoto Similarity\nJaccard/Tanimoto similarity measures overlap between fingerprint bit vectors:\n- **Range**: 0 to 1 (1 = identical, 0 = completely different)\n- **Symmetric**: similarity(A,B) = similarity(B,A)\n- **Formula**: |intersection| / |union|\n\n## Usage Example\n\n```python\nfrom rdkit import Chem\nfrom rdkit.Chem import AllChem\nfrom rdkit import DataStructs\n\ndef compute_tanimoto_similarity(smiles1, smiles2, radius=2, include_chirality=True):\n    \"\"\"Compute Tanimoto similarity between two molecules\"\"\"\n    mol1 = Chem.MolFromSmiles(smiles1)\n    mol2 = Chem.MolFromSmiles(smiles2)\n\n    if mol1 is None or mol2 is None:\n        return None  # Invalid SMILES\n\n    # Generate Morgan fingerprints\n    fp1 = AllChem.GetMorganFingerprintAsBitVect(\n        mol1,\n        radius=radius,\n        nBits=2048,\n        useChirality=include_chirality\n    )\n    fp2 = AllChem.GetMorganFingerprintAsBitVect(\n        mol2,\n        radius=radius,\n        nBits=2048,\n        useChirality=include_chirality\n    )\n\n    # Compute Tanimoto similarity\n    similarity = DataStructs.TanimotoSimilarity(fp1, fp2)\n    return similarity\n\n# Example usage\nsmiles_a = \"c1ccccc1\"  # benzene\nsmiles_b = \"c1ccccc1C\"  # toluene\nsimilarity = compute_tanimoto_similarity(smiles_a, smiles_b)\nprint(f\"Similarity: {similarity:.3f}\")  # Likely around 0.8\n```\n\n## Morgan Fingerprint Parameters\n\n### Radius Parameter\n- **radius=1**: Only immediate neighbors (fast, less specific)\n- **radius=2**: Two bonds away (good balance, default recommendation)\n- **radius=3**: Three bonds away (more specific, slower)\n\n### Chirality Parameter\n- **useChirality=True**: Distinguishes stereoisomers (important for drugs)\n- **useChirality=False**: Ignores stereochemistry (faster, less specific)\n\n### Bit Vector Size\n- **nBits=2048**: Default, good for most applications\n- **nBits=1024**: Faster, slightly less precise\n- **nBits=4096**: More detailed, slower\n\n## Batch Similarity Computation\n\n```python\ndef find_similar_molecules(target_smiles, candidate_smiles_list, radius=2,\n                          include_chirality=True):\n    \"\"\"Find similarity between target and multiple candidates\"\"\"\n    target_mol = Chem.MolFromSmiles(target_smiles)\n    if target_mol is None:\n        return []\n\n    target_fp = AllChem.GetMorganFingerprintAsBitVect(\n        target_mol,\n        radius=radius,\n        nBits=2048,\n        useChirality=include_chirality\n    )\n\n    similarities = []\n    for smiles in candidate_smiles_list:\n        mol = Chem.MolFromSmiles(smiles)\n        if mol is None:\n            continue\n\n        fp = AllChem.GetMorganFingerprintAsBitVect(\n            mol,\n            radius=radius,\n            nBits=2048,\n            useChirality=include_chirality\n        )\n\n        sim = DataStructs.TanimotoSimilarity(target_fp, fp)\n        similarities.append(sim)\n\n    return similarities\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n\n1. **SMILES Validation**: Always check if SMILES conversion is successful\n2. **Consistent Parameters**: Use same radius and chirality settings for all comparisons\n3. **Preprocessing**: Some SMILES may need sanitization\n4. **Error Handling**: Invalid SMILES return None from MolFromSmiles()\n\n## Performance Considerations\n\n- Computing fingerprints is fast (microseconds per molecule)\n- Tanimoto similarity is also very fast\n- For large datasets, pre-compute all fingerprints to avoid repeated calculations\n- Memory usage grows linearly with number of molecules\n\n## Common Issues\n\n1. **Invalid SMILES**: Ensure SMILES are well-formed\n2. **Inconsistent Results**: Use same parameters across all comparisons\n3. **Chirality Sensitivity**: Set useChirality based on your domain needs\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":4,"cache_creation_input_tokens":945,"cache_read_input_tokens":19723,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":945,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":24},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"8e28df1d-f990-4c35-90b6-476369423727","uuid":"45cb1893-8312-44d1-be28-eb738b7a0430"}

Morgan Fingerprints\nMorgan fingerprints are circular fingerprints that capture molecular connectivity information:\n- **Radius**: How many layers of atoms to consider (typical: 2-3)\n- **Chirality**: Whether to distinguish stereochemistry (important for pharmaceutical molecules)\n- **Bit Vector**: Binary representation of molecular features\n\n### 3. Tanimoto Similarity\nJaccard/Tanimoto similarity measures overlap between fingerprint bit vectors:\n- **Range**: 0 to 1 (1 = identical, 0 = completely different)\n- **Symmetric**: similarity(A,B) = similarity(B,A)\n- **Formula**: |intersection| / |union|\n\n## Usage Example\n\n```python\nfrom rdkit import Chem\nfrom rdkit.Chem import AllChem\nfrom rdkit import DataStructs\n\ndef compute_tanimoto_similarity(smiles1, smiles2, radius=2, include_chirality=True):\n    \"\"\"Compute Tanimoto similarity between two molecules\"\"\"\n    mol1 = Chem.MolFromSmiles(smiles1)\n    mol2 = Chem.MolFromSmiles(smiles2)\n\n    if mol1 is None or mol2 is None:\n        return None  # Invalid SMILES\n\n    # Generate Morgan fingerprints\n    fp1 = AllChem.GetMorganFingerprintAsBitVect(\n        mol1,\n        radius=radius,\n        nBits=2048,\n        useChirality=include_chirality\n    )\n    fp2 = AllChem.GetMorganFingerprintAsBitVect(\n        mol2,\n        radius=radius,\n        nBits=2048,\n        useChirality=include_chirality\n    )\n\n    # Compute Tanimoto similarity\n    similarity = DataStructs.TanimotoSimilarity(fp1, fp2)\n    return similarity\n\n# Example usage\nsmiles_a = \"c1ccccc1\"  # benzene\nsmiles_b = \"c1ccccc1C\"  # toluene\nsimilarity = compute_tanimoto_similarity(smiles_a, smiles_b)\nprint(f\"Similarity: {similarity:.3f}\")  # Likely around 0.8\n```\n\n## Morgan Fingerprint Parameters\n\n### Radius Parameter\n- **radius=1**: Only immediate neighbors (fast, less specific)\n- **radius=2**: Two bonds away (good balance, default recommendation)\n- **radius=3**: Three bonds away (more specific, slower)\n\n### Chirality Parameter\n- **useChirality=True**: Distinguishes stereoisomers (important for drugs)\n- **useChirality=False**: Ignores stereochemistry (faster, less specific)\n\n### Bit Vector Size\n- **nBits=2048**: Default, good for most applications\n- **nBits=1024**: Faster, slightly less precise\n- **nBits=4096**: More detailed, slower\n\n## Batch Similarity Computation\n\n```python\ndef find_similar_molecules(target_smiles, candidate_smiles_list, radius=2,\n                          include_chirality=True):\n    \"\"\"Find similarity between target and multiple candidates\"\"\"\n    target_mol = Chem.MolFromSmiles(target_smiles)\n    if target_mol is None:\n        return []\n\n    target_fp = AllChem.GetMorganFingerprintAsBitVect(\n        target_mol,\n        radius=radius,\n        nBits=2048,\n        useChirality=include_chirality\n    )\n\n    similarities = []\n    for smiles in candidate_smiles_list:\n        mol = Chem.MolFromSmiles(smiles)\n        if mol is None:\n            continue\n\n        fp = AllChem.GetMorganFingerprintAsBitVect(\n            mol,\n            radius=radius,\n            nBits=2048,\n            useChirality=include_chirality\n        )\n\n        sim = DataStructs.TanimotoSimilarity(target_fp, fp)\n        similarities.append(sim)\n\n    return similarities\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n\n1. **SMILES Validation**: Always check if SMILES conversion is successful\n2. **Consistent Parameters**: Use same radius and chirality settings for all comparisons\n3. **Preprocessing**: Some SMILES may need sanitization\n4. **Error Handling**: Invalid SMILES return None from MolFromSmiles()\n\n## Performance Considerations\n\n- Computing fingerprints is fast (microseconds per molecule)\n- Tanimoto similarity is also very fast\n- For large datasets, pre-compute all fingerprints to avoid repeated calculations\n- Memory usage grows linearly with number of molecules\n\n## Common Issues\n\n1. **Invalid SMILES**: Ensure SMILES are well-formed\n2. **Inconsistent Results**: Use same parameters across all comparisons\n3. **Chirality Sensitivity**: Set useChirality based on your domain needs\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}

PDFs\n- pdfplumber is slightly slower but more accurate\n- Cache extracted text if processing multiple times\n- For very large PDFs, process page-by-page\n\n## Example Workflow\n\n```python\nimport tuple sort\n    )\n```\n\n## Example Implementations\n\n### Scenario: Top K Similar Chemicals\n\n```python\ndef get_top_k_similar(chemical_name, similarity_dict, top_k=5):\n    \"\"\"\n    Get top k similar chemicals with proper sorting\n\n    Args:\n        chemical_name: Target chemical name\n        similarity_dict: {chemical_name: similarity_score}\n        top_k: Number of results to return\n\n    Returns:\n        List of tuples: [(name, similarity), ...]\n    \"\"\"\n    # Filter out the target chemical itself\n    candidates = [\n        (name, score)\n        for name, score in similarity_dict.items()\n        if name != chemical_name\n    ]\n\n    # Sort by score (descending), then by name (ascending)\n    sorted_results = sorted(\n        candidates,\n        key=lambda x: (-x[1], x[0])\n    )\n\n    return sorted_results[:top_k]\n```\n\n### Handling Ties in Results\n\n```python\ndef format_results_with_ranking(results_list):\n    \"\"\"\n    Format results with proper ranking (handles ties)\n\n    Results format: [(name, similarity), ...]\n    \"\"\"\n    formatted = []\n    current_rank = 1\n    previous_score = None\n\n    for idx, (name, score) in enumerate(results_list):\n        # If score differs from previous, update rank\n        if previous_score is not None and score != previous_score:\n            current_rank = idx + 1\n\n        formatted.append({\n            'rank': current_rank,\n            'name': name,\n            'similarity': score\n        })\n        previous_score = score\n\n    return formatted\n\n# Example output:\n# [\n#   {'rank': 1, 'name': 'compound_a', 'similarity': 0.95},\n#   {'rank': 1, 'name': 'compound_b', 'similarity': 0.95},  # Tied\n#   {'rank': 3, 'name': 'compound_c', 'similarity': 0.87},\n# ]\n```\n\n## Efficient Sorting for Large Datasets\n\n### Using heapq for Top K\n\n```python\nimport heapq\n\ndef get_top_k_efficient(similarity_scores, top_k):\n    \"\"\"\n    Efficiently get top k items from large dataset\n\n    Args:\n        similarity_scores: List of (name, score) tuples\n        top_k: Number of top items to return\n\n    Returns:\n        Sorted list of top k items\n    \"\"\"\n    # heapq.nlargest returns largest elements\n    top_k_items = heapq.nlargest(\n        top_k,\n        similarity_scores,\n        key=lambda x: (x[1], -ord(x[0][0]))  # Score desc, name asc\n    )\n    return top_k_items\n```\n\n### Stable Sorting for Consistent Results\n\n```python\ndef stable_sort_with_tiebreak(items, score_key, name_key):\n    \"\"\"\n    Stable sort that maintains alphabetical order for ties\n\n    Args:\n        items: List of dictionaries with score and name\n        score_key: Key for similarity score\n        name_key: Key for chemical name\n    \"\"\"\n    # First sort by name (for stable tiebreaking)\n    items.sort(key=lambda x: x[name_key])\n\n    # Then sort by score (stable sort preserves order for equal scores)\n    items.sort(key=lambda x: x[score_key], reverse=True)\n\n    return items\n```\n\n## Real-World Example\n\n```python\ndef rank_similar_molecules(target_name, molecules_dict, similarities_list, top_k):\n    \"\"\"\n    Complete ranking workflow\n\n    Args:\n        target_name: Name of target molecule\n        molecules_dict: {index: chemical_name}\n        similarities_list: List of (index, similarity_score) tuples\n        top_k: Number of results to return\n\n    Returns:\n        List of (chemical_name, similarity) tuples, sorted properly\n    \"\"\"\n    # Convert indices to names and filter out target\n    results = [\n        (molecules_dict[idx], score)\n        for idx, score in similarities_list\n        if molecules_dict[idx] != target_name\n    ]\n\n    # Sort: descending by similarity, then alphabetically\n    sorted_results = sorted(\n        results,\n        key=lambda x: (-x[1], x[0])\n    )\n\n    # Return top k\n    return sorted_results[:top_k]\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n\n1. **Consistent Tie Resolution**: Always use same criteria for ties\n2. **Negative Numbers for Reverse Sort**: Use `-x[1]` instead of reverse=True for tuple sorting\n3. **Alphabetical Secondary Sort**: Deterministic and easy to verify\n4. **Stable Sorting**: Python's sort is stable; use to advantage\n5. **Test Edge Cases**: Empty lists, single item, all equal scores\n\n## Testing Tie Resolution\n\n```python\ndef test_tiebreaking():\n    \"\"\"Verify tiebreaking works correctly\"\"\"\n    results = [\n        ('compound_b', 0.95),\n        ('compound_a', 0.95),\n        ('compound_c', 0.87),\n        ('compound_d', 0.95),\n    ]\n\n    sorted_results = sorted(\n        results,\n        key=lambda x: (-x[1], x[0])\n    )\n\n    # Expected: compound_a, compound_b, compound_d (all 0.95, alphabetical)\n    # then compound_c (0.87)\n    assert sorted_results[0] == ('compound_a', 0.95)\n    assert sorted_results[1] == ('compound_b', 0.95)\n    assert sorted_results[2] == ('compound_d', 0.95)\n    assert sorted_results[3] == ('compound_c', 0.87)\n```\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":4,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1550,"cache_read_input_tokens":22116,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":1550,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":25},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"8e28df1d-f990-4c35-90b6-476369423727","uuid":"4fd4f736-ba25-4315-a9d5-15e602d84f4c"}

If score differs from previous, update rank\n        if previous_score is not None and score != previous_score:\n            current_rank = idx + 1\n\n        formatted.append({\n            'rank': current_rank,\n            'name': name,\n            'similarity': score\n        })\n        previous_score = score\n\n    return formatted\n\n# Example output:\n# [\n#   {'rank': 1, 'name': 'compound_a', 'similarity': 0.95},\n#   {'rank': 1, 'name': 'compound_b', 'similarity': 0.95},  # Tied\n#   {'rank': 3, 'name': 'compound_c', 'similarity': 0.87},\n# ]\n```\n\n## Efficient Sorting for Large Datasets\n\n### Using heapq for Top K\n\n```python\nimport heapq\n\ndef get_top_k_efficient(similarity_scores, top_k):\n    \"\"\"\n    Efficiently get top k items from large dataset\n\n    Args:\n        similarity_scores: List of (name, score) tuples\n        top_k: Number of top items to return\n\n    Returns:\n        Sorted list of top k items\n    \"\"\"\n    # heapq.nlargest returns largest elements\n    top_k_items = heapq.nlargest(\n        top_k,\n        similarity_scores,\n        key=lambda x: (x[1], -ord(x[0][0]))  # Score desc, name asc\n    )\n    return top_k_items\n```\n\n### Stable Sorting for Consistent Results\n\n```python\ndef stable_sort_with_tiebreak(items, score_key, name_key):\n    \"\"\"\n    Stable sort that maintains alphabetical order for ties\n\n    Args:\n        items: List of dictionaries with score and name\n        score_key: Key for similarity score\n        name_key: Key for chemical name\n    \"\"\"\n    # First sort by name (for stable tiebreaking)\n    items.sort(key=lambda x: x[name_key])\n\n    # Then sort by score (stable sort preserves order for equal scores)\n    items.sort(key=lambda x: x[score_key], reverse=True)\n\n    return items\n```\n\n## Real-World Example\n\n```python\ndef rank_similar_molecules(target_name, molecules_dict, similarities_list, top_k):\n    \"\"\"\n    Complete ranking workflow\n\n    Args:\n        target_name: Name of target molecule\n        molecules_dict: {index: chemical_name}\n        similarities_list: List of (index, similarity_score) tuples\n        top_k: Number of results to return\n\n    Returns:\n        List of (chemical_name, similarity) tuples, sorted properly\n    \"\"\"\n    # Convert indices to names and filter out target\n    results = [\n        (molecules_dict[idx], score)\n        for idx, score in similarities_list\n        if molecules_dict[idx] != target_name\n    ]\n\n    # Sort: descending by similarity, then alphabetically\n    sorted_results = sorted(\n        results,\n        key=lambda x: (-x[1], x[0])\n    )\n\n    # Return top k\n    return sorted_results[:top_k]\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n\n1. **Consistent Tie Resolution**: Always use same criteria for ties\n2. **Negative Numbers for Reverse Sort**: Use `-x[1]` instead of reverse=True for tuple sorting\n3. **Alphabetical Secondary Sort**: Deterministic and easy to verify\n4. **Stable Sorting**: Python's sort is stable; use to advantage\n5. **Test Edge Cases**: Empty lists, single item, all equal scores\n\n## Testing Tie Resolution\n\n```python\ndef test_tiebreaking():\n    \"\"\"Verify tiebreaking works correctly\"\"\"\n    results = [\n        ('compound_b', 0.95),\n        ('compound_a', 0.95),\n        ('compound_c', 0.87),\n        ('compound_d', 0.95),\n    ]\n\n    sorted_results = sorted(\n        results,\n        key=lambda x: (-x[1], x[0])\n    )\n\n    # Expected: compound_a, compound_b, compound_d (all 0.95, alphabetical)\n    # then compound_c (0.87)\n    assert sorted_results[0] == ('compound_a', 0.95)\n    assert sorted_results[1] == ('compound_b', 0.95)\n    assert sorted_results[2] == ('compound_d', 0.95)\n    assert sorted_results[3] == ('compound_c', 0.87)\n```\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}

    include_chirality: bool = True):\n    \"\"\"\n    Compute Morgan fingerprint from a SMILES string.\n\n    Args:\n        smiles: SMILES representation of the molecule\n        radius: Radius of   Args:\n        fp1: First Morgan fingerprint\n        fp2: Second Morgan fingerprint\n\n    Returns:\n        Tanimoto similarity score (0-1), or None if computation fails\n    \"\"\"\n    try:\n        if fp1 is None or fp2 is None:\n            return None\n        similarity = DataStructs.TanimotoSimilarity(fp1, fp2)\n        return similarity\n    except Exception as e:\n molecule_pool_filepath: str,\n                                       top_k: int) -> List[Tuple[str, float]]:\n    \"\"\"\n    Find the top K chemically similar compounds to a target molecule.\n\n    This function:\n    1. Extracts chemical names from the PDF pool file\n    2. Converts target and pool chemicals to SMILES format (via PubChem)\n    3. Computes Morgan fingerprints (radius=2, with chirality)\n    4. Calculates Tanimoto similarity to each pool compound\n    5. Returns top K results sorted by similarity (descending) then alphabetically\n\n    Args:\n        target_molecule_name: Name of the target chemical compound\n        molecule_pool_filepath: Path to PDF file containing pool of chemicals\n        top_k: Number of similar compounds to return\n\n    Returns:\n        List of (chemical_name, similarity_score) tuples, sorted by:\n        - Similarity score (descending)\n        - Chemical name (alphabetically ascending) for ties\n    \"\"\"\n    # Step 1: Extract chemical names from PDF\n    pool_chemicals = extract_chemical_names_from_pdf(molecule_pool_filepath)\n    print(f\"Extracted {len(pool_chemicals)} chemicals from pool\")\n\n    # Step 2: Get SMILES for target molecule\n    print(f\"Fetching SMILES for target: {target_molecule_name}\")\n    target_smiles = get_smiles_from_pubchem(target_molecule_name)\n    if target_smiles is None:\n        print(f\"Error: Could not find SMILES for {target_molecule_name}\")\n        return []\n\n    print(f\"Target SMILES: {target_smiles}\")\n\n    # Get SMILES for pool chemical\n        smiles = get_smiles_from_pubchem(chemical_name)\n        if smiles is None:\n            continue\n\n        # Compute fingerprint\n        pool_fp = compute_morgan_fingerprint(smiles)\n        if pool_fp is None:\n            continue\n\n        # This function:\n    1. Includes retry logic with exponential\n    backoff for network resilience.\n\n    Args:\n        chemical_name: Name of the chemical compound\n        max_retries: Maximum number of retry attempts\n        backoff_factor: Backoff factor for exponential backoff\n\n    Returns:\n        SMILES string if found, None otherwise\n    \"\"\"\n    for attempt in range(max_retries):\n        try:\n            # Step 1: Get CID (Compound ID) from chemical name\n            encoded_name = quote(chemical_name)\n            search_url = f\"https://pubchem.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/rest/v1/compound/name/{encoded_name}/cids/json\"\n            search_response = requests.get(search_url, timeout=10)\n\n            # Handle 404 gracefully - compound not found\n            if search_response.status_code == 404:\n                return None\n\n            search_response.raise_for_status()\n\n            response_data = search_response.json()\n            if \"IdentifierList\" not in response_data:\n                return None\n\n            cids = response_data[\"IdentifierList\"].get(\"CID\", [])\n            if not cids:\n                return None\n\n            # Use the first (most relevant) CID\n            cid = cids[0]\n\n            # Step 2: Get SMILES from CID\n            props_url = f\"https://pubchem.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/rest/v1/compound/{cid}/property/CanonicalSMILES/json\"\n            props_response = requests.get(props_url, timeout=10)\n\n            if props_response.status_code == 404:\n                return None\n\n            props_response.raise_for_status()\n\n            props_data = props_response.json()\n            if \"PropertyTable\" not in props_data:\n                return None\n\n            compounds = props_data[\"PropertyTable\"].get(\"Compounds\", [])\n            if not compounds:\n                return None\n\n            smiles = compounds[0].get(\"CanonicalSMILES\")\n            return smiles\n\n        except requests.exceptions.Timeout:\n            if attempt < max_retries - 1:\n                wait_time = backoff_factor * (2 ** attempt)\n                print(f\"Timeout fetching SMILES for '{chemical_name}', retrying in {wait_time}s...\")\n                time.sleep(wait_time)\n            continue\n\n        except requests.exceptions.ConnectionError:\n            if attempt < max_retries - Use the first (most relevant) CID\n            cid = cids[0]\n\n            # Step 2: Get SMILES from CID\n            props_url = f\"https://pubchem.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/rest/v1/compound/{cid}/property/CanonicalSMILES/json\"\n            props_response = requests.get(props_url, timeout=10)\n\n            if props_response.status_code == 404:\n                return None\n\n            props_response.raise_for_status()\n\n            props_data = props_response.json()\n            if \"PropertyTable\" not in props_data:\n                return None\n\n            compounds = props_data[\"PropertyTable\"].get(\"Compounds\", [])\n            if not compounds:\n                return None\n\n            smiles = compounds[0].get(\"CanonicalSMILES\")\n            return smiles\n\n        except requests.exceptions.Timeout:\n            if attempt < max_retries - 1:\n                wait_time = backoff_factor * (2 ** attempt)\n                print(f\"Timeout fetching SMILES for '{chemical_name}', retrying in {wait_time}s...\")\n                time.sleep(wait_time)\n            continue\n\n        except requests.exceptions.ConnectionError:\n            if attempt < max_retries - 1:\n                wait_time = backoff_factor * (2 ** attempt)\n                print(f\"Connection error fetching SMILES for '{chemical_name}', retrying in {wait_time}s...\")\n                time.sleep(wait_time)\n            continue\n\n        except Exception as e:\n            # For other exceptions, don't retry\n            print(f\"Error fetching SMILES for '{chemical_name}': {type(e).__name__}: {e}\")\n            return None\n\n    print(f\"Failed to fetch SMILES for '{chemical_name}' after {max_retries} retries\")\n    return None\n\n\ndef compute_morgan_fingerprint(smiles: str, radius: int = 2,\n                               include_chirality: bool = True):\n    \"\"\"\n    Compute Morgan fingerprint from a SMILES string.\n\n    Args:\n        smiles: SMILES representation of the molecule\n        radius: Radius of the Morgan fingerprint (default: 2)\n        include_chirality: Whether to include chirality information (default: True)\n\n    Returns:\n        Morgan fingerprint as a bit vector, or None if SMILES is invalid\n    \"\"\"\n    try:\n        mol = Chem.MolFromSmiles(smiles)\n        if mol is None:\n            return None\n\n        try:\n            # Try using newer MorganGenerator API (RDKit >= 2023.03)\n            from rdkit.Chem import MorganGenerator\n            gen = MorganGenerator(radius=radius, includeChirality=include_chirality)\n            fp = gen.GetCountFP(mol)\n        except (ImportError, AttributeError):\n            # Fall back to older API\n            fp = AllChem.GetMorganFingerprintAsBitVect(\n                mol,\n                radius=radius,\n                nBits=2048,\n                useChirality=include_chirality\n            )\n        return fp\n    except Exception as e:\n        print(f\"Error computing fingerprint for SMILES '{smiles}': {e}\")\n        return None\n\n\ndef compute_tanimoto_similarity(fp1, fp2) -> Optional[float]:\n    \"\"\"\n    Compute Tanimoto similarity between two Morgan fingerprints.\n\n    Args:\n        fp1: First Morgan fingerprint\n        fp2: Second Morgan fingerprint\n\n    Returns:\n        Tanimoto similarity score (0-1), or None if computation fails\n    \"\"\"\n    try:\n        if fp1 is None or fp2 is None:\n            return None\n        similarity = DataStructs.TanimotoSimilarity(fp1, fp2)\n        return similarity\n    except Exception as e:\n        print(f\"Error computing Tanimoto similarity: {e}\")\n        return None\n\n\ndef topk_tanimoto_similarity_molecules(target_molecule_name: str,\n                                       molecule_pool_filepath: str,\n                                       top_k: int) -> List[Tuple[str, float]]:\n    \"\"\"\n    Find the top K chemically similar compounds to a target molecule.\n\n    This function:\n    1. Extracts chemical names from the PDF pool file\n    2. Converts target and pool chemicals to SMILES format (via PubChem)\n    3. Computes Morgan fingerprints (radius=2, with chirality)\n    4. Calculates Tanimoto similarity to each pool compound\n    5. Returns top K results sorted by similarity (descending) then alphabetically\n\n    Args:\n        target_molecule_name: Name of the target chemical compound\n        molecule_pool_filepath: Path to PDF file containing pool of chemicals\n        top_k: Number of similar compounds to return\n\n    Returns:\n        List of (chemical_name, similarity_score) tuples, sorted by:\n        - Similarity score (descending)\n        - Chemical name (alphabetically ascending) for ties\n    \"\"\"\n    # Step 1: Extract chemical names from PDF\n    pool_chemicals = extract_chemical_names_from_pdf(molecule_pool_filepath)\n    print(f\"Extracted {len(pool_chemicals)} chemicals from pool\")\n\n    # Step 2: Get SMILES for target molecule\n    print(f\"Fetching SMILES for target: {target_molecule_name}\")\n    target_smiles = get_smiles_from_pubchem(target_molecule_name)\n    if target_smiles is None:\n        print(f\"Error: Could not find SMILES for {target_molecule_name}\")\n        return []\n\n    print(f\"Target SMILES: {target_smiles}\")\n\n    # Step 3: Compute target fingerprint\n    target_fp = compute_morgan_fingerprint(target_smiles)\n    if target_fp is None:\n        print(f\"Error: Could not compute fingerprint for {target_molecule_name}\")\n        return []\n\n    # Step 4: Compute similarities for all pool chemicals\n    similarities: Dict[str, float] = {}\n\n    for chemical_name in pool_chemicals:\n        # Skip the target molecule itself\n        if chemical_name.lower() == target_molecule_name.lower():\n            continue\n\n        # Get SMILES for pool chemical\n        smiles = get_smiles_from_pubchem(chemical_name)\n        if smiles is None:\n            continue\n\n        # Compute fingerprint\n        pool_fp = compute_morgan_fingerprint(smiles)\n        if pool_fp is None:\n            continue\n\n        # Compute Tanimoto similarity\n        similarity = compute_tanimoto_similarity(target_fp, pool_fp)\n        if similarity is not None:\n            similarities[chemical_name] = similarity\n\n    # Step 5: Sort and return top K\n    # Sort by similarity (descending), then by name (ascending) for ties\n    sorted_results = sorted(\n        similarities.items(),\n        key=lambda x: (-x[1], x[0])\n    )\n\n    top_k_results = sorted_results[:top_k]\n    return top_k_results\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    # Example usage\n    pdf_path = \"/root/molecules.pdf\"\n\n    # Test with a sample chemical\n    target = \"Benzene\"\n    top_k = 5\n\n    print(f\"Finding top {top_k} chemicals similar to {target}\")\n    print(\"-\" * 50)\n\n    results = topk_tanimoto_similarity_molecules(target, pdf_path, top_k)\n\n    print(\"\\nResults:\")\n    for i, (chemical_name, similarity) in enumerate(results, 1):\n        print(f\"{i}. {chemical_name}: {similarity:.4f}\")\n","structuredPatch":[{"oldStart":146,"oldLines":19,"newStart":146,"newLines":14,"lines":["         if mol is None:","             return None"," ","-        try:","-            # Top K Similar Chemicals Finder\n\nA Python solution for finding the top K chemically similar compounds to a target molecule using Morgan fingerprints and Tanimoto similarity metrics.\n\n## Overview\n\nThis solution implements molecular similarity searching using:\n- **Chemical Name Resolution**: PubChem API for converting chemical names to SMILES format\n- **Molecular Fingerprints**: Morgan Fingerprints\nGenerates Morgan fingerprints with:\n- **Radius**: 2 (captures molecular features within 2 bonds)\n- **Chirality**: Enabled (distinguishes stereoisomers)\n- **Bit Vector Size**: 2048 bits (standard for similarity calculations)\n\n### 4. Similarity Computation\nCalculates Tanimoto similarity between fingerprint bit vectors:\n- **Formula**: |intersection| / |union|\n- **Range**: 0 (completely different) to 1 (identical)\n- **Metric**: Symmetric (similarity(A,B) = similarity(B,A))\n\n### 5. Sorting and Ranking\nResults are sorted using a two-level sort key:\n1. **Primary**: Similarity score (descending)\n2. **Secondary**: Chemical name (alphabetically ascending for ties)\n\nThis ensures deterministic, reproducible results.\n\n## Technical Specifications\n\n### Morgan Fingerprint Parameters\n```python\nAllChem.GetMorganFingerprintAsBitVect(\n    mol,\n    radius=2,           # Radius parameter (required)\n    nBits=2048,         # Bit vector size\n    useChirality=True   # Include stereochemistry\n)\n```\n\n### Tanimoto Similarity\n```python\nDataStructs.TanimotoSimilarity(fp1, fp2)\n```\n\nReturns a float between 0 and 1, where:\n- 1.0 = identical molecules\n- 0.0 = completely different molecules\n\n## Project Structure\n\n```\n/root/workspace/\n├── solution.py          # `molecule_pool_filepath`: Path to PDF file containing pool of chemicals\n- `top_k`: Number of similar compounds to return\n\n**Returns:**\n- List of tuples: `(chemical_name, similarity_score)`\n- Results sorted by similarity (descending), then alphabetically\n\n## Implementation Details\n\n### 1. PDF Text Extraction\nUses `pdfplumber` to extract chemical names from the PDF file. Each line in the PDF is treated as a separate chemical name.\n\n### 2. SMILES Conversion\nConverts chemical names to SMILES format using the PubChem REST API:\n- First, retrieves the Compound ID (CID) by searching the chemical name\n- Then, fetches the canonical SMILES from the CID\n- Includes retry logic with exponential backoff for network resilience\n\n### 3. Morgan Fingerprints\nGenerates Morgan fingerprints with:\n- **Radius**: 2 (captures molecular features within 2 bonds)\n- **Chirality**: Enabled (distinguishes stereoisomers)\n- **Bit Vector Size**: 2048 bits (standard for similarity calculations)\n\n### 4. Similarity Computation\nCalculates Tanimoto similarity between fingerprint bit vectors:\n- **Formula**: |intersection| / |union|\n- **Range**: 0 (completely different) to 1 (identical)\n- **Metric**: Symmetric (similarity(A,B) = similarity(B,A))\n\n### 5. Sorting and Ranking\nResults are sorted using a two-level sort key:\n1. **Primary**: Similarity score (descending)\n2. **Secondary**: Chemical name (alphabetically ascending for ties)\n\nThis ensures deterministic, reproducible results.\n\n## Technical Specifications\n\n### Morgan Fingerprint Parameters\n```python\nAllChem.GetMorganFingerprintAsBitVect(\n    mol,\n    radius=2,           # Radius parameter (required)\n    nBits=2048,         # Bit vector size\n    useChirality=True   # Include stereochemistry\n)\n```\n\n### Tanimoto Similarity\n```python\nDataStructs.TanimotoSimilarity(fp1, fp2)\n```\n\nReturns a float between 0 and 1, where:\n- 1.0 = identical molecules\n- 0.0 = completely different molecules\n\n## Project Structure\n\n```\n/root/workspace/\n├── solution.py          # Step 1: Get CID (Compound ID) from chemical name\n            encoded_name = quote(chemical_name)\n            search_url = f\"https://pubchem.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/rest/v1/compound/name/{encoded_name}/cids/json\"\n            search_response = requests.get(search_url, timeout=10)\n\n            # Handle 404 gracefully - compound not found\n            if search_response.status_code == 404:\n                return None\n\n            search_response.raise_for_status()\n\n            response_data = search_response.json()\n            if \"IdentifierList\" not in response_data:\n                return None\n\n            cids = response_data[\"IdentifierList\"].get(\"CID\", [])\n            if not cids:\n                return None\n\n            # Use the first (most relevant) CID\n            cid = cids[0]\n\n            # Step 2: Get SMILES from CID\n            props_url = f\"https://pubchem.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/rest/v1/compound/{cid}/property/CanonicalSMILES/json\"\n            props_response = requests.get(props_url, timeout=10)\n\n            if props_response.status_code == 404:\n                return None\n\n            props_response.raise_for_status()\n\n            props_data = props_response.json()\n            if \"PropertyTable\" not in props_data:\n                return None\n\n            compounds = props_data[\"PropertyTable\"].get(\"Compounds\", [])\n            if not compounds:\n                return None\n\n            smiles = compounds[0].get(\"CanonicalSMILES\")\n            return smiles\n\n        except requests.exceptions.Timeout:\n            if attempt < max_retries - 1:\n                wait_time = backoff_factor * (2 ** attempt)\n                print(f\"Timeout fetching SMILES for '{chemical_name}', retrying in {wait_time}s...\")\n                time.sleep(wait_time)\n            continue\n\n        except requests.exceptions.ConnectionError:\n            if attempt < max_retries - 1:\n                wait_time = backoff_factor * (2 ** attempt)\n                print(f\"Connection error fetching SMILES for '{chemical_name}', retrying in {wait_time}s...\")\n                time.sleep(wait_time)\n            continue\n\n        except Exception as e:\n            # For other exceptions, don't retry\n            print(f\"Error fetching SMILES for '{chemical_name}': {type(e).__name__}: {e}\")\n            return None\n\n    print(f\"Failed to fetch SMILES for '{chemical_name}' after {max_retries} retries\")\n    return None\n\n\ndef compute_morgan_fingerprint(smiles: str, radius: int = 2,\n                               include_chirality: bool = True):\n    \"\"\"\n    Compute Morgan fingerprint from a SMILES string.\n\n    Args:\n        smiles: SMILES representation of the molecule\n        radius: Radius of the Morgan fingerprint (default: 2)\n        include_chirality: Whether to include chirality information (default: True)\n\n    Returns:\n        Morgan fingerprint as a bit vector, or None if SMILES is invalid\n    \"\"\"\n    try:\n        mol = Chem.MolFromSmiles(smiles)\n        if mol is None:\n            return None\n\n        # Use GetMorganFingerprintAsBitVect for standard bit vector fingerprints\n        # This is the most common and compatible version for similarity calculations\n        fp = AllChem.GetMorganFingerprintAsBitVect(\n            mol,\n            radius=radius,\n            nBits=2048,\n            useChirality=include_chirality\n        )\n        return fp\n    except Exception as e:\n        print(f\"Error computing fingerprint for SMILES '{smiles}': {e}\")\n        return None\n\n\ndef compute_tanimoto_similarity(fp1, fp2) -> Optional[float]:\n    \"\"\"\n    Compute Tanimoto similarity between two Morgan fingerprints.\n\n    Args:\n        fp1: First Morgan fingerprint\n        fp2: Second Morgan fingerprint\n\n    Returns:\n        Tanimoto similarity score (0-1), or None if computation fails\n    \"\"\"\n    try:\n        if fp1 is None or fp2 is None:\n            return None\n        similarity = DataStructs.TanimotoSimilarity(fp1, fp2)\n        return similarity\n    except Exception as e:\n        print(f\"Error computing Tanimoto similarity: {e}\")\n        return None\n\n\ndef topk_tanimoto_similarity_molecules(target_molecule_name: str,\n                                       molecule_pool_filepath: str,\n                                       top_k: int) -> List[Tuple[str, float]]:\n    \"\"\"\n    Find the top K chemically similar compounds to a target molecule.\n\n    This function:\n    1. Extracts chemical names from the PDF pool file\n    2. Converts target and pool chemicals to SMILES format (via PubChem)\n    3. Computes Morgan fingerprints (radius=2, with chirality)\n    4. Calculates Tanimoto similarity to each pool compound\n    5. Returns top K results sorted by similarity (descending) then alphabetically\n\n    Args:\n        target_molecule_name: Name of the target chemical compound\n        molecule_pool_filepath: Path to PDF file containing pool of chemicals\n        top_k: Number of similar compounds to return\n\n    Returns:\n        List of (chemical_name, similarity_score) tuples, sorted by:\n        - Similarity score (descending)\n        - Chemical name (alphabetically ascending) for ties\n    \"\"\"\n    # Step 1: Extract chemical names from PDF\n    pool_chemicals = extract_chemical_names_from_pdf(molecule_pool_filepath)\n    print(f\"Extracted {len(pool_chemicals)} chemicals from pool\")\n\n    # Step 2: Get SMILES for target molecule\n    print(f\"Fetching SMILES for target: {target_molecule_name}\")\n    target_smiles = get_smiles_from_pubchem(target_molecule_name)\n    if target_smiles is None:\n        print(f\"Error: Could not find SMILES for {target_molecule_name}\")\n        return []\n\n    print(f\"Target SMILES: {target_smiles}\")\n\n    # Step 3: Compute target fingerprint\n    target_fp = compute_morgan_fingerprint(target_smiles)\n    if target_fp is None:\n        print(f\"Error: Could not compute fingerprint for {target_molecule_name}\")\n        return []\n\n    # Step 4: Compute similarities for all pool chemicals\n    similarities: Dict[str, float] = {}\n\n    for chemical_name in pool_chemicals:\n        # Skip the target molecule itself\n        if chemical_name.lower() == target_molecule_name.lower():\n            continue\n\n        # Get SMILES for pool chemical\n        smiles = get_smiles_from_pubchem(chemical_name)\n        if smiles is None:\n            continue\n\n        # Compute fingerprint\n        pool_fp = compute_morgan_fingerprint(smiles)\n        if pool_fp is None:\n            continue\n\n        # Compute Tanimoto similarity\n        similarity = compute_tanimoto_similarity(target_fp, pool_fp)\n        if similarity is not None:\n            similarities[chemical_name] = similarity\n\n    # Step 5: Sort and return top K\n    # Sort by similarity (descending), then by name (ascending) for ties\n    sorted_results = sorted(\n        similarities.items(),\n        key=lambda x: (-x[1], x[0])\n    )\n\n    top_k_results = sorted_results[:top_k]\n    return top_k_results\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    # Example usage\n    pdf_path = \"/root/molecules.pdf\"\n\n    # Test with a sample chemical\n    target = \"Benzene\"\n    top_k = 5\n\n    print(f\"Finding top {top_k} chemicals similar to {target}\")\n    print(\"-\" * 50)\n\n    results = topk_tanimoto_similarity_molecules(target, pdf_path, top_k)\n\n    print(\"\\nResults:\")\n    for i, (chemical_name, similarity) in enumerate(results, 1):\n        print(f\"{i}. {chemical_name}: {similarity:.4f}\")\n","numLines":276,"startLine":1,"totalLines":276}}}
